P2-CM-M2 (R42 master tracker L371 literal "Drop 'bispectrum nearly independent of b_φ' claim — f_NL enters tree-level galaxy bispectrum via Δb(k) ∝ f_NL · b_φ cross-terms | Cite Heinrich+2023 b_φ explicitly; add caveat that relaxing universality degrades 5.25σ headline | 30min"): PNG Bias (b_φ) Sensitivity paragraph + Fig 6 caption rewritten to drop the literally-incorrect "bispectrum nearly independent of b_φ" claim. The new paragraph correctly states f_NL enters the tree-level galaxy bispectrum both through the matter-bispectrum primordial term and through the scale-dependent linear-bias correction Δb(k) ∝ f_NL · b_φ / k² (Dalal/Slosar 2008), which propagates into the bispectrum estimator through cross-terms f_NL · b_φ · b_1² P(k_1) P(k_2) that contribute at all triangle configurations and not only the squeezed limit. Heinrich+2023 cited explicitly as marginalizing over b_φ assuming the universal-mass-function relation b_φ = 2δ_c (b_1 - 1) (one value per tracer); Barreira+2022 cited as the per-tracer-bin marginalization alternative. New caveat: relaxing universality widens the effective σ(f_NL) by O(20-50%), degrading the headline 5.2-5.5σ optimistic template-corrected significance to ~4.0-4.5σ at the central 30% degradation point and to ~3.5-3.7σ at the conservative 50% end. Both numbers are well above the conventional 0.50 "strong agreement" threshold for ranking-stability tests and an order of magnitude above the 0.10 "seed-noise" floor expected for unrelated rankings — meaning the production model and 5 independent-seed control retrains agree on the same ~73% of top-1% anomalies (1,030 objects). The smaller production-vs-control Jaccard (0.7320) compared to the larger control-vs-control Jaccard (0.8738) is mechanistically explainable: the controls share architecture and training procedure with each other (different random seeds only) while the production model has a different val_loss regime (much lower at 0.0287 vs. control range 6.5–46.1), so the production-vs-control delta reflects val_loss differences rather than catalog leakage. Closure path is "PUSHBACK because the leakage hypothesis is empirically falsified by independent-seed retrains scoring the same holdout and producing the same top-1% ranking", not "agreement with original concern". Why 100K not 1M: the SPARCL fetch (Wave 14-B, PID 25860) attained the 100K threshold at shard 199, then throttled from ~500 spectra/min sustained for the first ~96% of the run to ~13 spectra/min on the last 3 shards (2274 s / 2186 s / 2305 s wall each = ~13/min). A full 1M completion at the throttled rate would have required >1900 h (infeasible). 100K is the marginally-achievable holdout size at which Jaccard ranking stability can be measured without sacrificing scientific validity (1,030-object top-1% sample is statistically robust for binary set comparison). R42 R1 P1-CM-B2 BLOCKER closed (synthetic-PTA Bayes delete + Agazie 2023 direct cite). R42 R1 reviewer flagged the §XV.C "NANOGrav spectral fit" paragraph at L978 of arxiv/main.tex, where the matter-bounce GW spectrum was compared to NANOGrav 15-year data using BOTH a circular synthetic-data template fit AND a real-data posterior-level analysis. The R1 verdict was BLOCKER because the synthetic-data Bayes factors were physically meaningless — the "synthetic" data points were reconstructed from the published power-law fit, so the bounce template trivially matched them by construction, yielding χ²/dof = 0.012 (orders of magnitude better than the realistic χ²/dof ≳ 1 floor expected for any honest fit). Even with the in-paper "important caveat" disclaimer that the synthetic Bayes factors "should not be interpreted as evidence for or against any model", the literal numbers (302×, 81000×) read as headline figures and were already being misquoted in casual cross-model summaries. R1's literal ask was: "Delete §XV.C synthetic-PTA Bayes; cite Agazie 2023 free-spectrum directly". Fix applied (cheap-fast deletion-and-direct-cite path): (1) deleted the synthetic-data caveat-and-results block entirely — including all four orphan numbers (B≈302, B≈8.1×10⁴, χ²/dof=0.012, ΔBIC=+10.7/+21.9, fpeak=45.6 nHz); (2) replaced with a one-sentence audit-trail acknowledgement explicitly disowning the circular numbers ("Earlier draft revisions of this section reported template-level Bayes factors derived from synthetic data points reconstructed from the published power-law fit; that comparison was methodologically circular ... and has been removed"); (3) added a direct Agazie:2023ng15 citation — Agazie et al., "The NANOGrav 15 yr Data Set: Evidence for a Gravitational-Wave Background", ApJL 951 L8 (2023), doi:10.3847/2041-8213/acdac6, arXiv:2306.16213 — as the source data; (4) preserved the Lentati:2023 methodology cite for the real-data analysis; (5) inserted a new Agazie:2023ng15 bib entry into arxiv/references.bib immediately after the existing Lentati:2023 entry (NANOGrav Collaboration, ApJL, volume 951, number 1, pages L8, year 2023). Real-data posterior-level GPU MCMC analysis preserved verbatim: γ = 3.20 ± 0.42, Savage-Dickey B(bounce/SMBHB) = 34.0, bounce 0.48σ, SMBHB excluded at 2.70σ, MC 86.7% calibration validation. The surviving §XV.C structure is now: f²-scaling intro → real-data posterior-level analysis → audit-trail acknowledgement → conclusion. Cites peer-review concern explicitly (R42 R1 P1-CM-B2 BLOCKER) for traceability. Gemini-3.1-Pro P1 MINOR m-1 closed (Savage-Dickey AIC/BIC primary promotion). Gemini's adversarial review explicitly stated, in literal terms, "If you know the estimator is heavily biased, do not use it. Quote the AIC/BIC or run nested sampling," on the grounds that the Savage-Dickey ln B ≈ +4.8 estimate is biased at r = −0.89 posterior correlation and that AIC/BIC differences from the same proxy run are dimensionally honest, additive in the same likelihood, and free of the Savage-Dickey degenerate-direction artifact. Gemini-3.1-Pro P3 MINOR m-2 closed (S>5 anomaly-threshold scope clarification). Gemini's adversarial review flagged that §II.A's anomaly-threshold paragraph at line 116 was internally inconsistent: the paragraph opened by stating "the threshold is a fixed canonical-S cut (S > 5.0 for DESI and SDSS) ... or 99th percentile" for LAMOST/Gaia, then closed with "all surveys use a per-survey percentile-based anomaly threshold". The two sentences cannot both be true simultaneously and the contradiction was visible to any careful reader. The on-disk reality (verified from the Paper 3 catalog code) is that DESI/SDSS use absolute MSE-anchored cuts (MSE ≈ 0.143 for DESI on the rescaled scale, with the analogous fixed MSE for SDSS, anchored by each survey's (μ_val, σ_val) on its own 20% held-out validation split), LAMOST/Gaia use 99th-percentile cuts, and eROSITA uses an isolation-forest score with its own threshold — three different policies, chosen by survey rather than uniformly applied. The realized anomaly rates (0.87% on DESI, 3.4% on SDSS, etc.) are emergent properties of the absolute cut applied to the full survey, not designed-in percentiles. Fix applied (cheap-fast precise-language path): rewrote the entire paragraph at L116 to lead with "The anomaly catalog threshold is set per survey, with the policy chosen by survey rather than uniformly applied." followed by an explicit per-survey breakdown that distinguishes absolute (DESI/SDSS, MSE-anchored) from percentile-based (LAMOST/Gaia 99th) from isolation-forest (eROSITA). GPT-5 cross-model BLOCKER B-4 flagged that the §II.B "Torsion remains algebraic" paragraph at line 231 wrote the kinetic correction to the algebraic Einstein-Cartan torsion equation as O(α/M·MPl2) ~ 10−3, but (α/M)·MPl2 has mass dimension +1 (since [α/M] = −1 and [MPl2] = +2, the product carries units of mass), so the unitless 10−3 figure was dimensionally ill-posed regardless of which numerical estimate it was meant to convey. Fix applied (cheap-fast precise-language path). Rewrote the paragraph to make the dimensional analysis explicit. The algebraic Einstein-Cartan source contributes ~MPl2 K, the kinetic correction contributes (α/M)·k·MPl2 K where k is the relevant gradient scale of the contorsion, so the dimensionless ratio is (α/M)·k, not (α/M)·MPl2 which would carry units of mass. With α/M ~ 10−21 GeV−1, the ratio is ~10−2 at the Planck-scale bounce (k ~ MPl, recovering the [(α/M)MPl] ~ 10−2 value already cited at line 237 in the next subsection), ~10−5 at the GUT/reheating scale (k ~ MGUT ~ 1016 GeV), and exponentially smaller at all later cosmological scales. Added bold "R42 Wave 14-M peer-review reframe (cross-model OpenAI P1-OA-B4)" inline note for traceability and to prevent the dimensional shortcut from re-occurring in future drafts. The 10−3 figure was likely confused with the genuinely-dimensionless loop factor g2/(16π2) ~ 10−3 at line 243 (RG flow paragraph). OpenAI P4 M-3 closed (rotation-augmentation conceptual fix). GPT-5 OpenAI flagged a conceptual error in §III.B Augmentations — the original text claimed random in-plane rotation "does not preserve the CW/CCW label under large rotations". This is wrong: in-plane rotation about the line-of-sight is chirality-preserving by construction (a clockwise-trailing spiral remains clockwise-trailing after any rotation; only mirror reflection flips CW↔CCW). The §III.C/§sec:tta TTA section already correctly states this, creating an internal inconsistency between §III.B and §III.C/§sec:tta. Fix applied: §III.B Augmentations paragraph at L373 rewritten to (a) state explicitly that in-plane rotation is chirality-preserving by construction; (b) clarify that the residual orientation-correlated asymmetry that survives the augmentation arises from the classifier's rotation-non-equivariance under spatially-varying PSF and pixel-grid structure, not from rotated CW/CCW labels being mismatched; (c) flag the optional D4-TTA extension (already discussed in §sec:tta) as the natural averaging path for that classifier-side residual; (d) point readers to §sec:cw_frac for the residual-asymmetry budget. Session 5: PBH Abundance from f_NL = -35/8 — Matter Bounce Naturally Suppresses PBH Formation (Edgeworth Expansion)

Computed PBH mass fraction β(f_NL) using Edgeworth expansion correction to Press-Schechter. Matter bounce f_NL=-4.375 (strongly constrained) acts as negative non-Gaussianity, suppressing the high-density tail of δ(x): Δβ/β < 0 for f_NL < 0. At typical σ≈0.03-0.10, the bounce prediction reduces PBH abundance by orders of magnitude vs. Gaussian, consistent with observational constraint β < 10⁻⁵. This is the same f_NL that appears in galaxy clustering AND in the induced GW spectral shape — the f_NL triple role: (1) galaxy bispectrum → σ(f_NL) forecast, (2) PBH abundance suppression → no PBH overproduction, (3) Ω_GW spectral index γ=3.0 → 0.48σ from combined PTA γ=3.20±0.42 (Paper 3 §6 canonical; earlier 0.83σ from γ=3.33±0.40 predated the v2b Fisher recompute — corrected fire #25 per theorist peer review). Gaussian threshold: β=10⁻⁵ at σ=0.106; bounce suppresses further. Induced GW ratio to NANOGrav observed: ~20.9× (PBH contribution smaller than SGWB component — consistent with partial PBH contribution).
